for some large enough constant
C_{4}>0
. Now let
t=\sqrt{6\log(n)}
,
we have that as long as
n\geq C_{2}\log N
for some large enough
constant
C_{2}
, with probability at least
1-2n^{-12}
,

\|\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\|\leq 4C_{4}\sigma\sqrt{n}.

Finally, (
23b
) is obtained by observing the symmetry
of Gaussian random variable.

C.2
Proof of Lemma
4

Recall that

\bm{B}_{k}\coloneqq\underbrace{\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}}_{\eqqcolon\bm{B}_{k,1}}+\underbrace{\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}}_{\eqqcolon\bm{B}_{k,2}}.

(28)

We define
\bm{B}_{k,1}
and
\bm{B}_{k,2}
as in (
28
).
In Section
C.2.1
and
C.2.2
, We will show that with probability
at least
1-O(KN^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{B}_{k,1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\cdot\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\min}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{K}}+\frac{\log N}{K}\right)

(29a)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{B}_{k,2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{nd_{k}\log N}{K}}+\frac{\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)\log N}{K}\right)

(29b)

Then the proof is completed by triangular inequality.

C.2.1
Proof of (
29a
)

We will use the truncated matrix Bernstein inequality to control
\|K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{B}_{k,1}\|
.
We first show that the following inequalities. Recall
r_{\mathrm{avg}}=K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}
.
For all
k\in[K]
, with probability at least
1-O(N^{-11})
, as
long as
n\geq C_{1}\log N
for some constant
C_{1}>0
,

\|\bm{B}_{k,1}\|\leq C_{2}\cdot\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\min}}

(30a)

and

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{B}_{k,1};\|\bm{B}_{k,1}\|>C_{2}\cdot\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right]\right\|=0.

(30b)

for some constant
C_{2}>0.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,1}\bm{B}_{k,1}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(r+r_{\mathrm{avg}})\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}

(30c)

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,1}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k,1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Kn\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}.

(30d)

The proof of these bounds is deferred to the end
of this section. Now invoking the truncated matrix Bernstein inequality
(see
[
CCFM21
]
, Corollary 3.2), we have that with
probability at least
1-O(KN^{-10})
,

\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{B}_{k}\right\|\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\min}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{K}}+\frac{\log N}{K}\right)

for some large enough constant
C_{3}>0
.

Proof of (
30a
) and (
30b
).

For each
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{B}_{k,1}\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right\|\leq\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|\left\|\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\right\|\left\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\right\|.

Recall that
\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}=\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}

so and
\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\|\leq 1
. Combining this with (
22
),
we have that with probability at least
1-O(N^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{B}_{k,1}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\cdot\frac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sigma_{\min}},

for some large enough constant
C_{1}>0
. For the truncated expectation,
similar to (
23b
), the symmetry of Gaussian random variable
implies that the expectation is
\bm{0}
.

Proof of (
30c
).

For each
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{B}_{k,1}\bm{B}_{k,1}^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}.

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{=}\sigma^{2}\mathrm{Trace}\left(\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\overset{\text{(ii)}}{=}\frac{(r+r_{k})\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}.

Here (i) follows from Lemma
1
and (ii) follows
from the fact

\mathrm{Trace}\left(\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)\leq\mathrm{rank}\left(\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)\cdot\left\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right\|\leq\frac{(r+r_{k})}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}.

Then combining triangular inequality with the fact that
\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\|\leq 1

finishes the proof.

Proof of (
30d
).

Expanding
\bm{B}_{k}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,1}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k,1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\right]\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{=}\sigma^{2}\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}(n-r-r_{k})\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}.

Here (i) follows from Lemma
1
. Then by triangular
inequality

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,1}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k,1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\|\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,1}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k,1}\right\|\leq K\cdot n\sigma^{2}\cdot\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\|=\frac{Kn\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}.

C.2.2
Proof of (
29b
)

We will use the truncated matrix Bernstein inequality to control
\|K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{B}_{k,2}\|
.
We first show the following inequalities. For each
k\in[K]
,
with probability at least
1-O(N^{-11})
, as long as
n\geq C_{1}\log N

for some constant
C_{12}>0
,

\|\bm{B}_{k,2}\|\leq\frac{C_{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}

(31a)

and

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{B}_{k,2};\|\bm{B}_{k,2}\|>\frac{C_{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right]\right\|\leq\frac{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}N^{-11}

(31b)

for some constant
C_{2}>0
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{B}_{k,2}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\sigma^{4}d(r+r_{\mathrm{avg}})}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}

(31c)

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,2}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k,2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\sigma^{4}dn}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}.

(31d)

The proof of these bounds is deferred to the end
of this section. Now invoking the truncated matrix Bernstein inequality
(see
[
CCFM21
]
, Corollary 3.2), with probability at
least
1-O(KN^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{B}_{k}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{nd\log N}{K}}+\frac{\left(\sqrt{nd}+n\right)\log N}{K}\right)

for some large enough constant
C_{3}>0
.

Proof of (
31a
) and (
31b
).

For each
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{B}_{k,2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\right\|\leq\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|\left\|\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right\|\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\right\|.

Recall that
\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}=\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}

and
\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}=\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}
.
Then
\|\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\|\leq 1/\sigma_{\min}^{2}
and
\|\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\|\leq 1
.
Combining this with (
22
), we have that
with probability at least
1-O(N^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{B}_{k,2}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\frac{\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}},

for some large enough constant
C_{1}>0
. For the truncated expectation,
by (
23b
), for some large enough constant
C_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{B}_{k,2}\|;\|\bm{B}_{k,2}\|>\frac{C_{1}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\right\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\|;\|\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\|>C_{1}\cdot\sigma\sqrt{n}\right]\leq\frac{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}N^{-11}.

Union bound on
k\in[K]
completes the proof.

Proof of (
31c
).

For each
k\in[K]
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{B}_{k,2}^{\top}\right]=\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}.

By Lemma
1
,
\mathbb{E}\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}=d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}
,
therefore we can simplify the equation above to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{B}_{k,2}^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}-\sigma^{4}d_{k}^{2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}

(32)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{4}\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{I}_{d_{k}})\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1})\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}.

Here the second equality follows from Lemma
2

and the fact that
\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}=\bm{0}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{B}_{k,2}^{\top}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{4}d_{k}(r+r_{k})\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\right\|=\sigma^{4}d_{k}(r+r_{k})\left\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-4}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right\|\leq\frac{\sigma^{4}d_{k}(r+r_{k})}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}.

Summing it up over
k\in[K]
with triangular inequality completes
the proof.

Proof of (
31d
).

Expanding
\bm{B}_{k}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bm{B}_{k,2}^{\top}\bm{B}_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{=}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{=}\sigma^{4}\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{I}_{d_{k}})\cdot\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp})\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}.

Here (i) uses the same simplification as (
32
) and (ii)
follows from Lemma
2
. Then

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{B}_{k,2}^{\top}\right]\right\|\leq\frac{\sigma^{4}d_{k}n}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}.

Summing it up over
k\in[K]
with triangular inequality completes
the proof.

C.3
Proof of Lemma
5

Suppose Lemma
3
and
4
hold.
Consider
\bm{I}_{n}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}
. It
is the top-
(n-r)
eigenspace of
\bm{I}_{n}-K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}
.
Using the decomposition in Lemma
3
, we have
that

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\bm{B}_{k}+\bm{B}_{k}^{\top}+\bm{R}_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}-\bm{\Delta}.

Then (
16
) imples
\|\bm{\Delta}\|\leq\theta/8.

Now consider

\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}=\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}

as the ground truth and
\bm{\Delta}
as the perturbation. Since

\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}\right\|=1-\theta,

the rank of
\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}

is
n-r
and its top-
(n-r)
subspace is
\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}
.
This and the fact that
\theta/8
allow us to invoke Theorem 1 in

[
Xia21
]
, and arrive at

\bm{I}_{n}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}=\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Delta}\bm{P}^{\perp}-\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{\Delta}\bm{P}^{-1}-\bm{R}

for some
\bm{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
such that
\|\bm{R}\|\leq 32\|\bm{\Delta}\|^{2}/\theta^{2}.

Then

\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}=\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{\Delta}\bm{P}^{\perp}+\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{\Delta}\bm{P}^{-1}+\bm{R}.

C.4
Proof of Lemma
6

Recall that

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\bm{B}_{k}+\bm{B}_{k}^{\top}+\bm{R}_{k}\right),

where

\displaystyle\bm{B}_{k}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}+\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bm{B}_{k,1}+\bm{B}_{k,2}

with
\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}=\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}

and
\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}=\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}
.
Moreover,

\bm{P}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top},\qquad\bm{P}^{-1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{P}^{\perp}=\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}.

Since

\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}=\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})=\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top})=\bm{0},

we have that for all
k\in[K]
,
\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}^{\perp}=\bm{0}
.
Then it suffices to control
K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k}\bm{P}^{\perp}

and
K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{R}_{k}\bm{P}^{\perp}
. In
the rest of this proof, we will show that with probability at least

1-O(KN^{-10})
, the following inequalities hold:

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{R}_{k}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\kappa^{2}\sigma^{2}n}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}(nd_{k}+n^{2})}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\right)

(33a)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{K}+\frac{r+r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{K\theta}+\frac{r\cdot r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{K\theta}\wedge\frac{r}{K^{2}\theta^{2}}}\right)\log^{5/2}N

(33b)

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{nd}{K}+\frac{d(r+r_{\mathrm{avg}})}{K\theta}+\left(nd+n^{2}\right)\left(\frac{3r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{K\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{K^{2}\theta^{2}}\right)}\log N.

(33c)

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
. These
combined imply the result of Lemma (
6
).

Proof of (
33a
).

Recall that Lemma
3
says that with probability
at least
1-O(KN^{-10})
, for any
k\in[K]
,

\|\bm{R}_{k}\|\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{\kappa^{2}\sigma^{2}n}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}(nd_{k}+n^{2})}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\right)

for some constant
C_{1}>0
. Then since
\mu_{i}\geq\theta
and
\bm{P}^{\perp}

is a projection,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{R}_{k}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\bm{P}^{-1}\|\cdot\|\bm{R}_{k}\|\cdot\|\bm{P}^{\perp}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\theta}\left(\frac{\kappa^{2}\sigma^{2}n}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}+\frac{\sigma^{4}(nd_{k}+n^{2})}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\right).

Proof of (
33b
).

Within the scope of this proof, for any
k\in[K]
, let

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{k,1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,1}\bm{P}^{\perp}=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{P}^{\perp}.

It is clear that
\bm{Y}_{k,1}
is zero-mean. We will prove (
33b
)
with truncated matrix Bernstein inequality. We first claim the following
inequalities hold. For some constant
C_{1}>0
, with probability
at least for each
k\in[K]
, with probability at least
1-O(N^{-11})
,

\|\bm{Y}_{k,1}\|\leq C_{1}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\left(\sqrt{\left(n+\frac{Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}+\frac{Kr\cdot r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{r}{\theta^{2}}\right)\log^{3}N}\right)

(34a)

and

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{Y}_{k,1};\|\bm{Y}_{k,1}\|>C_{1}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\left(\sqrt{\left(n+\frac{Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}+\frac{Kr\cdot r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{r}{\theta^{2}}\right)\log^{3}N}\right)\right]\right\|=0.

(34b)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{Y}_{k,1}\bm{Y}_{k,1}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{rK\sigma^{2}}{\theta\sigma_{\min}^{2}}

(34c)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{Y}_{k,1}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k,1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(n+\frac{r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\right)\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}.

(34d)

The identity (
34b
) holds since
Gaussian random variable is symmetric and the whole expectation is

\bm{0}
. We defer the proof of (
34a
), (
34c
)
and (
34d
) to Section
C.4.1
,
C.4.2
,
and
C.4.3
, respectively. Now invoking the truncated
matrix Bernstein inequality (see
[
CCFM21
]
, Corollary 3.2),
we have that with probability at least
1-O(KN^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|=\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{Y}_{k,1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{K}+\frac{r+r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{K\theta}+\frac{r\cdot r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{K\theta}\wedge\frac{r}{K^{2}\theta^{2}}}\right)\log^{5/2}N

for some constants
C_{4}>0
.

Proof of (
33c
).

Within the scope of this proof, for any
k\in[K]
, let

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{k,2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{P}^{\perp}=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\left(\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}.

By Lemma
1
,
\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}

is zero-mean. Then
\bm{Y}_{k,2}
is zero-mean as well. We will prove
(
33b
) with truncated matrix Bernstein inequality.
We first claim the following inequalities hold. For some constant

C_{1}>0
, with probability at least for each
k\in[K]
, with probability
at least
1-O(N^{-11})
,

\|\bm{Y}_{k,2}\|\leq\frac{C_{1}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}

(35a)

and

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{Y}_{k,2};\|\bm{Y}_{k,2}\|>\frac{C_{1}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}\right]\right\|\leq C_{4}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}N^{-11}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}.

(35b)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Krd\sigma^{4}}{\theta\sigma_{\min}^{4}}

(35c)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma^{4}}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}Kd\left(n+\frac{r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\right).

(35d)

The identity (
35b
) holds since
Gaussian random variable is symmetric and the whole expectation is

\bm{0}
. We defer the proof of (
35a
), (
35c
)
and (
35d
) to Section
C.4.1
,
C.4.2
,
and
C.4.3
, respectively. Now invoking the truncated
matrix Bernstein inequality (see
[
CCFM21
]
, Corollary 3.2),
we have that with probability at least
1-O(KN^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,2}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|=\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{nd}{K}+\frac{d(r+r_{\mathrm{avg}})}{K\theta}+\left(nd+n^{2}\right)\left(\frac{3r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{K\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{K^{2}\theta^{2}}\right)}\log N.

for some constants
C_{4}>0
.

Before we proceed with the proof of the these conditions, we present
several useful lemmas. The first one controls the number of
\mu_{i}

that is small and gives a upper bound on
\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}
.
The proof is deferred to Section
C.4.7
.

Lemma 7
.

Let
\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in[n-r]}

be defined as in (
18
). Then

\left|\{i:\mu_{i}\leq 1/2\}\right|\leq 2r_{\mathrm{avg}}

(36a)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}

\displaystyle=n-r-r_{\mathrm{avg}}

(36b)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 2n+\frac{2r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}.

(36c)

The second lemma concerns with the result on alignment of eigenvectors
of
\bm{P}
and
\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}
. We defer the proof to Section
C.4.8
.

Lemma 8
.

Recall
\bm{x}_{i}
is defined
by the eigen-decomposition
\bm{P}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}.

We claim that for every
k\in[K]
and
i\in[n-r]
,

\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i}\leq(K\mu_{i})\wedge 1,

(37a)

and

\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}.

(37b)

C.4.1
Proof of (
34a
)

Fix
k\in[K]
. Recall that

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{k,1}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,1}\bm{P}^{\perp}=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}.

\displaystyle=\sum_{ij}\underbrace{[\bm{E}_{k}]_{ij}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{i}\bm{e}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}}_{\eqqcolon\bm{S}_{ij}}.

We will bound
\|\bm{Y}_{k}\|
with truncated matrix Bernstein inequality.
We first claim that for each
(i,j)\in[n]\times[d_{k}]
, with probability
at least
1-O(N^{-14})
,

\|\bm{S}_{ij}\|\leq\frac{C_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log N}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}

(38a)

and

\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{S}_{ij};\|\bm{S}_{ij}\|>\frac{C_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log N}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}\right]\right\|=0.

(38b)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}\right)

(38c)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}^{\top}\bm{S}_{ij}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(4n+\frac{2Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\right).

(38d)

The equality (
38b
) follows from the
symmetry of Gaussian random variable. We defer the proof of the other
inequalities to the end of this section. Now invoking the truncated
matrix Bernstein inequality (see
[
CCFM21
]
, Corollary 3.2),
we have that with probability at least
1-O(N^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{Y}_{k}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\frac{\sigma}{\sigma_{\min}}\left(\sqrt{\left(n+\frac{Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}+\frac{Kr\cdot r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{r}{\theta^{2}}\right)\log^{3}N}\right)

for some constant
C_{2}>0
.

Proof of (
38a
).

Using Gaussian tail bound, we have that for each
(i,j)\in[n]\times[d_{k}]
,
with probability at least
1-N^{-14}
,

|[\bm{E}_{k}]_{ij}|\leq C_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log N}

for some constant
C_{1}>0
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\bm{S}_{ij}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left|[\bm{E}_{k}]_{ij}\right|\cdot\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\|\cdot\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log N}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}

for some constant
C_{1}>0
. The last inequality uses (
37b
)
in Lemma
8
.

Proof of (
38c
).

Consider
\mathbb{E}\sum_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}^{\top}
. We organize
it to be

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sum_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{E}_{k}]_{ij}^{2}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\left(\sum_{ij}\alpha_{j}\bm{e}_{i}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}\alpha_{j}\bm{I}_{n}\right)\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(\sigma^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}\alpha_{j}\right)\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1},

where

\alpha_{j}\coloneqq\bm{e}_{j}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{e}_{j}.

Since
\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}
is rank-
r
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}\alpha_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Trace}\left(\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)

\displaystyle\leq r\left\|\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq r\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-2}\|\leq\frac{r}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}.

Then with Lemma (
8
), we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}\right).

Proof of for (
38d
).

Within the scope of this proof we define
\bm{M}
with

\displaystyle\bm{M}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}\bm{e}_{j}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{i}\bm{e}_{j}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d_{k}}\bm{e}_{j}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{e}_{i}\right)\bm{e}_{j}^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)\bm{I}_{d_{k}}

To control this, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Trace}\left(\sum_{i}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-2}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-2}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{x}_{i}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\left(K\mu_{i}^{-1}\wedge\mu_{i}^{-2}\right).

The last inequality uses (
37a
).

By Lemma
7
,
\left|\{i:\mu_{i}\leq 1/2\}\right|\leq 2r_{\mathrm{avg}}
.
For any
i
such that
\mu_{i}\leq 1/2
,
K\mu_{i}^{-1}\wedge\mu_{i}^{-2}\leq K/\theta
,
otherwise
K\mu_{i}^{-1}\wedge\mu_{i}^{-2}\leq 4
. Therefore,

\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\left(K\mu_{i}^{-1}\wedge\mu_{i}^{-2}\right)\leq 4n+\frac{2Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}.

(39)

Combining these results, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sum_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}^{\top}\bm{S}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{M}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}

\displaystyle\preceq\sigma^{2}\left(4n+\frac{2Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\right)\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}

Then

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\sum_{ij}\bm{S}_{ij}^{\top}\bm{S}_{ij}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(4n+\frac{2Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\right).

C.4.2
Proof of (
34c
)

Recall

\bm{Y}_{k,1}\bm{Y}_{k,1}^{\top}=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{B}_{k,1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{B}_{k,1}^{\top}\bm{P}^{-1},

where
\bm{B}_{k,1}=\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}
.
Invoke Lemma
1
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,1}\bm{Y}_{k,1}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{P}^{\perp}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Trace}(\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{P}^{\perp}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}.

As
\bm{P}^{\perp}
is rank-
r
and
\|\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{k}^{\star-1}\|\leq\sigma_{\min}^{-1}
,

\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,1}\bm{Y}_{k,1}^{\top}\preceq\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}.

(40)

Summing up over
k
and substituting
\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}=\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}
,
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,1}\bm{Y}_{k,1}^{\top}

\displaystyle\preceq\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\bm{P}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{P}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right)\left(K\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{Kr\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right).

(41)

The first equality follows from the definition of
\bm{P}^{-1}
,

\{\mu_{i}\}
and
\{\bm{x}_{i}\}
. Then as
\min_{i}\mu_{i}\geq\theta
,

\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\bm{Y}_{k}\bm{Y}_{k}^{\top}]\right\|\leq\frac{Kr\sigma^{2}}{\theta\sigma_{\min}^{2}}.

C.4.3
Proof of (
34d
)

Let
\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an arbitrary vector such that
\|\bm{v}\|\leq 1
.
By linearity of expectation,

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{Y}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k}\right)\bm{v}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Trace}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{Y}_{k}\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k}^{\top}\right]\right).

Similar to (
41
), since
\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}
is rank
1 and
\|\bm{v}\|\leq 1
, we have that

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\bm{Y}_{k}\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k}^{\top}]\preceq\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right).

Therefore

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\top}\left(\mathbb{E}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\bm{Y}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k}\right)\bm{v}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Trace}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{Y}_{k}\bm{v}\bm{v}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k}^{\top}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Trace}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right)\cdot\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\cdot\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}.

To this end we take supremum over
\bm{v}
such that
\|\bm{v}\|\leq 1

and invoke the Lemma
7
to reach (
34d
).

C.4.4
Proof of (
35a
) and (
35b
)

Fix
k\in[K]
. By (
22a
) and (
37b
),
with probability at least
1-O(N^{-11})
, for some large enough constant

C_{1}
.

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{Y}_{k,2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|\left\|\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right\|\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}\cdot C_{1}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)\cdot\sigma_{\min}^{-2}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{1}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}\cdot

Moreover,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{Y}_{k,2};\|\bm{Y}_{k,2}\|>\frac{C_{1}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|\left\|\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\|\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\|;\|\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}-d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\|>C_{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\sqrt{nd_{k}}+n\right)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C_{2}\sigma^{2}N^{-10}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}}

for some large enough constant
C_{2}
.

C.4.5
Proof of (
35c
)

By Lemma
1
,
\mathbb{E}[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}]=d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}
.
Then we can simplify
\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}

as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}-d_{k}^{2}\sigma^{4}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}.

Now using Lemma
2
, we can simplify this to
be

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{4}d_{k}\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1})\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}\preceq\frac{rd_{k}\sigma^{4}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}.

The last line uses the fact that
\mathrm{rank}(\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1})\leq r

and
\|\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\|\leq\sigma_{\min}^{4}
.
Summing up over
k\in[K]
, by the definition of
\bm{P}
in (
17b
)
and
\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\coloneqq\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}
,
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}

\displaystyle\preceq\frac{rd\sigma^{4}}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\bm{P}^{-1}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)\bm{P}^{-1}=\frac{Krd\sigma^{4}}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\bm{P}^{-1}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{P}^{-1}=\frac{Krd\sigma^{4}}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\bm{P}^{-1}.

Then

\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}\right\|\leq\frac{Krd\sigma^{4}}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\|\bm{P}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{Krd\sigma^{4}}{\theta\sigma_{\min}^{4}}.

C.4.6
Proof of (
35d
)

By Lemma
1
,
\mathbb{E}[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}]=d_{k}\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}
.
Then we can simplify
\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k,2}

as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}-d_{k}^{2}\sigma^{4}\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{E}_{k}\bm{E}_{k}^{\top}\right]\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}.

Now using Lemma
2
and (
39
),
we can simplify the summation over
k
be

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sigma^{4}d_{k}\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\bm{P}^{-2}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp})\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}

\displaystyle\preceq\sigma^{4}d\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{P}^{-2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right)\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{4}d\mathrm{Trace}\left(K\bm{P}^{-1}\right)\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}

The last line uses the definition of
\bm{P}
in (
17b
)
and
\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\coloneqq\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}
.
Now invoke Lemma
7
, we have that

\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{P}^{-1}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\leq 2n+\frac{2r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}.

Then we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bm{Y}_{k,2}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{k,2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{4}Kd\left(2n+\frac{2r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\right)\left\|\bm{P}^{\perp}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{-1}\bm{P}^{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma^{4}}{\sigma_{\min}^{4}}Kd\left(2n+\frac{2r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\right).

C.4.7
Proof of Lemma
7

We prove (
36b
), (
36a
), and (
36c
)
in order.

For (
36b
), recall that

\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}.

We take the trace of both sides. For the left hand side,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{Trace}\left(\bm{I}_{n_{1}}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(n-r-r_{k}\right)=n-r-r_{\mathrm{avg}}.

The last line follows from the fact that
\bm{I}_{n_{1}}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}

has eigenvalue 1 with
n-r-r_{k}
algorithmic multiplicity. For the
right hand side, since the trace equals the sum of eigenvalues,

\mathrm{Trace}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}.

Combining the two identities yields (
36b
).

For (
36a
), let
\alpha\coloneqq\left|\{i:\mu_{i}\leq 1/2\}\right|
.
Since
\mu_{i}\leq 1
, then

n-r-r_{\mathrm{avg}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}\geq n-r-\left|\{i:\mu_{i}\leq 1/2\}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|\{i:\mu_{i}\leq 1/2\}\right|.

Therefore
\alpha\leq 2r_{\mathrm{avg}}
.

Finally we prove (
36c
). For all
i
such
that
\mu_{i}\leq 1/2
, we use the upper bound
\mu_{i}^{-1}\leq 1/\theta
,
otherwise we use
\mu_{i}^{-1}\leq 2
, then

\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\leq\frac{2r_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}+2n.

C.4.8
Proof of Lemma
8

We start with the proof of (
37a
). As
\bm{x}_{i}

is a unit vector and
\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}

is a projection,

\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{x}_{i}\leq 1.

By the definition of
\bm{x}_{i}
,

\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{j}^{\star}\bm{U}_{j}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{x}_{i}=\mu_{i}.

Then since
\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{x}_{k}\geq 0

for all
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{K}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{x}_{i}\leq\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\frac{1}{K}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{j}^{\star}\bm{U}_{j}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{x}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mu_{i}.

Combining the two inequalities we have (
37a
).

Now we consider (
37b
). Let
\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

be a unit vector. Let
\alpha_{i}\coloneqq\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{v}
.
Since
\{\bm{x}_{i}\}
is a basis of
\mathrm{col}(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top})
,

\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}^{2}\leq 1
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\|\leq\sqrt{\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\left(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top}\right)\bm{x}_{i}}\leq\sqrt{K\mu_{i}}.

(42)

We also have that

\displaystyle\bm{P}^{-1}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{v}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\mu_{i}^{-1}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\mu_{i}}\bm{x}_{i}.

Therefore

\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{v}\right\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\frac{\alpha_{i}^{2}}{\mu_{i}^{2}}.

As
\mu_{i}\geq\theta
, we immediately has

\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{v}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\alpha_{i}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}.

By Lemma
7
, we have that
|\{i:\mu_{i}\leq 1/2\}|\leq 2r_{\mathrm{avg}}
.
Then for all
i
such that
\mu_{i}\leq 1/2
,
we have the upper bound
\alpha_{i}^{2}/\mu_{i}^{2}\leq K/\mu_{i}\leq K/\theta
from (
42
). Otherwise we use
\mu_{1}>1/2
to get
\alpha_{i}^{2}/\mu_{i}^{2}\leq 4\alpha_{i}^{2}
.
Then

\sum_{i=1}^{n-r}\frac{\alpha_{i}^{2}}{\mu_{i}^{2}}\leq\frac{2Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}+1\leq\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}.

Combining the two bounds and taking supremum over
\bm{v}
, we have
that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}\bm{P}_{k}^{\perp}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bm{v}:\|\bm{v}\|\leq 1}\left\|\bm{P}^{-1}(\bm{I}_{n}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star\top})\bm{v}\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{3Kr_{\mathrm{avg}}}{\theta}\wedge\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
2

In this section, we prove a stronger version of Theorem
2
which allows for unbalanced matrices, i.e., the number of columns can be much larger than the number
n
of rows.

Theorem 5
.

Suppose that
\theta\leq 1/2
,
r\geq 8
, and
n\geq 6r
. Moreover, suppose
d\geq C_{1}\log(K/r)+C_{2}
for some large enough constant
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Then we have

\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\{\bm{A}_{k}^{\star}\}\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\|\geq\frac{\sigma}{20\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{K}+\frac{r}{K\theta}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{50\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{nd}{K}+\frac{rd}{K\theta}}.

(43)

The infimum on
\widehat{\bm{U}}
is taken over all measurable functions of the data.

Compared to the vanilla lower bound in Theorem
2
, Theorem
5
has an additional term that captures the second-order dependency on the noise level
\sigma
. More importantly, it is identical to two terms in the upper bound in Theorem
4
.

D.1
Proof of Theorem
5

Assume without loss of generality that

\sigma_{\min}=1
. Consider a random realization of
\bm{A}_{k}^{\star}
given fixed
\bm{U}^{\star},\{\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}
using the following procedure:

1.

Draw
\bm{V}^{\star}_{k}
and
\bm{W}^{\star}_{k}
to be independent random matrices with i.i.d. Gaussian entries of variance
1/d
. Let
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}=\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}+\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}
and
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}=\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}+\bm{E}_{k}
.

2.

Take
\bm{A}_{k}^{\star}=\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}
and
\bm{A}_{k}=\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}+\bm{E}_{k}
if
\sigma_{2r}(\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star})\geq 0.9
, and re-draw Step 1 otherwise.

We will show in Section
D.2
that the KL-divergence between two truncated instances is close to the KL-divergence between their non-truncated counterpart.

By construction this randomly generated
\{\bm{A}_{k}^{\star}\}
lies in
\Theta
as long as
\{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}
satisfies (
10a
) and (
10b
). Therefore

\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\{\bm{A}_{k}^{\star}\}\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\|\geq\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\|,

where
\Theta^{\prime}
is all
\{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}
that satisfies (
10a
) and (
10b
). Here the first expectation is taken w.r.t.
\{\bm{E}_{k}\}
and the second expectation is taken w.r.t.
{\{\bm{E}_{k},\bm{V}_{k}^{\star},\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}\}}
.

It now suffices to prove the following bounds:

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma}{20}\sqrt{\frac{n}{K}}

(44a)

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{20}\sqrt{\frac{nd}{K}}

(44b)

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma}{50}\sqrt{\frac{r}{K\theta}}

(44c)

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\{\bm{U},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{50}\sqrt{\frac{rd}{K\theta}}.

(44d)

We prove the first two bounds in Section
D.3
and the last two in Section
D.4
.

D.2
KL-divergence between truncated instances

Consider two sets of subspace parameters
(\bm{U}^{\star},\{\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\})
and
({\bm{U}}^{\star^{\prime}},\{{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star^{\prime}}\})
.
We use
[\cdot]_{\bm{U}^{\star}}
or
[\cdot]_{{\bm{U}}^{\star^{\prime}}}
to distinguish the matrices associated with different parameters.
Fix
k\in[K]
and consider
\bm{A}_{k}^{\star}
. We denote the law of
[\bm{A}_{k}]_{\bm{U}^{\star}}
as
\mathbb{P}_{k}
and its probability density to be
P_{k}(\cdot)
.
Similarly we denote the law of
[\bm{A}_{k}]_{{\bm{U}}^{\star^{\prime}}}
as
{\mathbb{P}}^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}
and its probability density to be
{P}^{{}^{\prime}}_{k}(\cdot)
.
On the other hand, we denote the law of
[\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}]_{\bm{U}^{\star}}
as
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}
and its probability density to be
\widetilde{{P}}_{k}
. Similarly we denote the law of
[\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}]_{\bm{U}^{\star^{\prime}}}
as
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{\prime}
and its probability density to be
\widetilde{{P}}_{k}^{\prime}
. We then have the following lemma.

Lemma 9
.

Let
k\in[K]
, suppose
(\bm{U}^{\star},\{\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\})
and
({\bm{U}}^{\star^{\prime}},\{{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star^{\prime}}\})
satisfy (
10a
) and (
10b
). Then

\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{k}\parallel{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{\prime})\leq\mathrm{KL}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{\prime})+4e^{-C_{1}d},

where
C_{1}>0
is a universal constant.

D.2.1
Proof of Lemma
9

We first present a lemma that shows the probability that
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}
is rejected is small. The proof is deferred to Section
D.2.2
.

Lemma 10
.

Let
k\in[K]
, suppose
(\bm{U}^{\star},\{\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\})
and
({\bm{U}}^{\star^{\prime}},\{{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\star^{\prime}}\})
satisfy (
10a
) and (
10b
). Then

\mathbb{P}\left[\sigma_{2r}(\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star})<0.9\right]\leq 2\exp(-C_{1}d)

for some universal constant
C_{1}
.

Given the generation process, it is straightforward to see that for
any matrix of the form
\bm{B}=\bm{U}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}+\bm{U}_{k}\bm{W}^{\top}
,
we have

\tilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})=\frac{P_{k}(\bm{B})1\{\sigma_{2r}(\bm{B})\geq 0.9\}}{\int P_{k}(\bm{B})1\{\sigma_{2r}(\bm{B})\geq 0.9\}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}}.

As a result, we obtain the inequalities

\tilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})\leq\frac{P_{k}(\bm{B})}{\mathbb{P}_{k}(\sigma_{2r}(\bm{B})\geq 0.9)}\leq\frac{P_{k}(\bm{B})}{1-2e^{-C_{1}d}}.

Moreover, if
\sigma_{2r}(\bm{B})\geq 0.9
, we have
P_{k}(\bm{B})\leq\tilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})
.

Now consider the KL-divergence
\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{k}\parallel{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{\prime})
, using the inequalities above, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{k}\parallel{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\sigma_{2r}(\bm{B})\geq 0.9}P_{k}(\bm{B})\log\frac{P_{k}(\bm{B})}{P_{k}^{\prime}(\bm{B})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\sigma_{2r}(\bm{B})\geq 0.9}\widetilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})\log\frac{\widetilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})}{(1-2e^{-C_{1}d})\widetilde{P}_{k}^{\prime}(\bm{B})}

\displaystyle\leq\int\widetilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})\log\frac{\widetilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})}{\widetilde{P}_{k}^{\prime}(\bm{B})}+\int_{\sigma_{2r}(\bm{B})\geq 0.9}\widetilde{P}_{k}(\bm{B})\log\frac{1}{1-2e^{-C_{1}d}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{KL}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{\prime})-\log(1-2e^{-C_{1}d}).

It remains to control the auxiliary term. As long as
d\geq 2/C_{1}
,

-\log(1-2e^{-C_{1}d})\leq 4e^{-C_{1}d}.

The proof is now completed.

D.2.2
Proof of Lemma
10

Each column of
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}
can be written as
\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{v}+\bm{U}^{\star}_{k}\bm{w}
where
\bm{v},\bm{w}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{r}/d)
. Therefore these columns are i.i.d. Gaussian vectors with mean 0 and covariance
(\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top})/d
. It is then equivalent to write
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}
as
\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\bm{Z}^{\top}
, where
\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}
is a
n\times 2r
matrix such that
\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}=\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}
, and
\bm{Z}
is a
d\times 2r
random matrix whose entries are i.i.d. Gaussian with mean 0 and variance
1/d
.

It now suffices to control the minimum singular value of
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star\top}=\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}
, which has expectation
\bm{I}_{2r}
.
By (4.22) in
[
Ver18
]
, for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}-\bm{I}_{2r}\right\|\geq C_{1}\cdot\frac{1}{d}\left(\sqrt{rd}+t\sqrt{d}+r+t^{2}\right)\right]\leq 2\exp(-2t^{2}),

(45)

where
C_{1}>0
is some large enough constant. Set
t=C_{2}\sqrt{d}
for some small enough constant
C_{2}>0
, we have that with probability at least

1-2e^{-2C_{2}^{2}d}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}\right\|=\left\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\left(\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}-\bm{I}_{2r}\right)\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}\right\|\leq\left\|\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}-\bm{I}_{2r}\right\|\leq 0.19.

By Weryl’s inequality,

\sigma_{2r}(\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star\top})\geq 1-\left\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}-\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}^{\top}\right\|\geq 0.81

and

\sigma_{2r}(\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star})=\sqrt{\sigma_{2r}(\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star}\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}^{\star\top})}\geq 0.9.

D.3
Proof of (
44a
) and (
44b
)

We employ the generalized Fano’s method. We construct a hypotheses class
\Gamma\subset\Theta
,
where each
\gamma\in\Gamma
represents a hypothesis consisting
of
\bm{U}^{\star},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}

for
k\in[K]
. We also assume
\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}
and
\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}
to be sampled with the procedure described in Section
D.1
. We use subscript
\gamma
(e.g.
[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}]_{\gamma}
)
to denote the elements associated with the hypothesis

\gamma
. We also use
\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma)}
to denote the probability
distribution of
\{\bm{A}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
induced by (
3
).

Let
\epsilon>0
be a scalar to be specified later.
We will construct a hypothesis class
\Gamma
of cardinality
|\Gamma|\geq e^{nr/6}

such that for all
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma
, if
\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}
,

\left\|\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{1}}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{2}}\right\|\geq\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{\sqrt{2r}}

(46)

and

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}Kd\epsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}+4Ke^{-C_{2}d},

(47)

where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are some universal constants. We will construct
\Gamma

in Section
D.3.1
and prove (
46
) and (
47
) in Section
D.3.2
.

We now select two different
\epsilon
to prove (
44a
) and (
44b
).

Proof of (
44a
).

Let
\epsilon=\sqrt{rn\sigma^{2}/(24C_{1}^{2}K)}
. For any
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma
such that
\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}
, as long as
d\geq C_{2}^{-1}\log(192K/nr)
,

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}Kd\epsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}d}+4Ke^{-C_{2}d}\leq\frac{nr}{48}+4Ke^{-C_{2}d}\leq\frac{\log|\Gamma|}{4}.

Meanwhile,

\left\|\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{1}}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{2}}\right\|\geq\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{\sqrt{2r}}\geq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{n}{24K}}.

Then by generalized Fano method (Lemma 3 in
[
Yu97
]
), as long
as
|\Gamma|\geq 16
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma}{2}\sqrt{\frac{n}{24K}}\left(1-\frac{\log|\Gamma|/4+\log 2}{\log|\Gamma|}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma}{20}\sqrt{\frac{n}{K}}.

Proof of (
44b
).

Let
\epsilon=\sigma^{2}\sqrt{rnd/(24C_{1}^{2}K)}
. For any
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma
such that
\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}
, as long as
d\geq C_{2}^{-1}\log(192K/nr)
,

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)\leq\frac{C_{1}^{2}Kd\epsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}d\cdot\sigma^{2}d}+4Ke^{-C_{2}d}\leq\frac{nr}{48}+4Ke^{-C_{2}d}\leq\frac{\log|\Gamma|}{4}.

Meanwhile,

\left\|\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{1}}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{2}}\right\|\geq\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{\sqrt{2r}}\geq\sigma^{2}\sqrt{\frac{nd}{24K}}.

Then by generalized Fano method (Lemma 3 in
[
Yu97
]
), as long
as
|\Gamma|\geq 16
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{nd}{24K}}\left(1-\frac{\log|\Gamma|/4+\log 2}{\log|\Gamma|}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{20}\sqrt{\frac{nd}{K}}.

D.3.1
Instance construction

Without loss of generality, assume
n
is divisible by 3 and
K

is even. For each
\gamma\in\Gamma
, we will associate it with an
orthogonal matrix
\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n/3\times r}
. Let
\bm{X}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n/3\times r}

be an arbitrary orthogonal matrix, i.e.,
\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\bm{X}_{0}=\bm{I}_{r}
.
Consider the Frobenius norm on the projection matrix, i.e., for any
orthogonal matrices
\bm{X}_{1}
and
\bm{X}_{2}
,
d(\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2})=\|\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.
Let

B_{d}(\bm{X}_{0},\epsilon)\coloneqq\left\{\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{n/3\times r}:\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=\bm{I}_{r},d(\bm{X},\bm{X}_{0})\leq\epsilon\right\}.

Then Lemma 1 in
[
CMW13
]
implies that for some small enough
constant
C_{1}>0
, the packing number
satisfies

\mathcal{M}(B_{d}(\bm{X}_{0},\epsilon),C_{1}\epsilon,d)\geq e^{r(n/3-r)}\geq e^{rn/6}.

The last inequality comes from the assumption that
6r\leq n
. By the definition of a packing number, there exists a set
A_{\epsilon}\subset B_{d}(\bm{X}_{0},\epsilon)

with
|A_{\epsilon}|\geq e^{nr/6}
such that for all
\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2}\in A_{\epsilon}
,
if
\bm{X}_{1}\neq\bm{X}_{2}
,

\|\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}\|\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2r}}\|\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{\sqrt{2r}}.

(48)

For any
\bm{X}
, we design a
\gamma
uniquely associated with
it. We construct the hypothesis set
\Gamma
by doing this for all

\bm{X}\in A_{\epsilon}
.

Construction of
\gamma
.

Fix
\bm{X}\in A_{\epsilon}
. For the shared component, let
\bm{U}^{\star}
,
\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}
and
\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}
be

\bm{U}^{\star}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\bm{0}_{n/3\times r}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\bm{0}_{n/3\times r}\end{bmatrix}\text{,}\qquad\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{0}_{n/3\times r}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\sqrt{1-\theta}\bm{X}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\sqrt{\theta}\bm{X}\end{bmatrix}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{0}_{n/3\times r}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\sqrt{1-\theta}\bm{X}\vskip 5.0pt\\
-\sqrt{\theta}\bm{X}\end{bmatrix}.

We then let
\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}=\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}
for all odd
k

and
\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}=\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}
for all even
k
.

We now verify the first two conditions in (
10
):

1.

Orthogonality (
10a
): Since
\bm{U}^{\star}\in B_{d}(\bm{X}_{0},\epsilon)
, we have

\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{U}^{\star}=\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=\bm{I}_{r}
.
For any
k\in[K]
,

\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}=\bm{0}_{n/3\times r}^{\top}\bm{0}_{n/3\times r}+(1-\theta)\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}+\theta\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}=\bm{I}_{r},

and
\bm{U}^{\star\top}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}=\bm{0}_{r\times r}
.

2.

Misalignment (
10b
): Compute the sum of outer products:

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}\bm{U}_{+}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}\bm{U}_{-}^{\star\top}

\displaystyle=2\begin{bmatrix}\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&(1-\theta)\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}&\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&\theta\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

Then

\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}\right\|=\left\|\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}\bm{U}_{+}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}\bm{U}_{-}^{\star\top}\right)\right\|=\theta\vee(1-\theta)=1-\theta.

D.3.2
Proof of (
46
) and (
47
)

Let
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
be two hypotheses and let
\bm{X}_{1}
,

\bm{X}_{2}
be the corresponding matrces in
A_{\epsilon}
. For
the separation gap (
46
), it follows directly from (
48
) that

\left\|\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{1}}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{2}}\right\|=\left\|\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}\right\|\geq\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{\sqrt{2r}}.

We now turn to the KL-divergence bound (
47
). For
i=1,2
, let
\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(\gamma_{i})}
be the
law of
\bm{A}_{k}
under
\gamma_{i}
and
\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{i})}

be the joint law of
\{\bm{A}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
under
\gamma_{i}
. Due to independence and symmetry, we have

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)=K\cdot\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right).

(49)

Moreover, for
i=1,2
, let
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{(\gamma_{i})}
be the
law of
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}
under
\gamma_{i}
as defined by the sampling procedure in Section
D.1
. Lemma
9
shows that

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)\leq\mathrm{KL}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)+4e^{-C_{2}d},

(50)

where
C_{2}
is a universal constant.

It now suffices to compute
\mathrm{KL}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma_{2})})
.
Observe that the
j
-th column of
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}
is

[\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}]_{\cdot,j}=\bm{U}^{\star}([\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}]_{j,\cdot})^{\top}+\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}([\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}]_{j,\cdot})^{\top}+[\bm{E}_{k}]_{\cdot,j}.

Recall that
\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}
and
\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}
are entrywise i.i.d. Gaussian with covariance
1/d
and
\bm{E}_{k}
are entrywise i.i.d. Gaussian with covariance
1
. Then each column of
\bm{A}_{k}
is independent with

[\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}]_{\cdot,j}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\bm{0},\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}/d+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}/d+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right)

for all
j\in[d]
.
As the covariance of each column is the same, we may denote it as

\bm{\Sigma}_{i}\coloneqq\sigma^{2}\left[\bm{I}_{n}+\frac{\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}}{\sigma^{2}d}\right]_{\gamma_{i}}

for
i=1,2
. It is also easy to see that

\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\left[\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}}{\sigma^{2}d+1}\right]_{\gamma_{i}}.

By independence and the KL-divergence of multivariate Gaussian distributions,

\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma_{2})})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left(\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{1})-n-\log\frac{\mathrm{det}(\bm{\Sigma}_{1})}{\mathrm{det}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2})}\right).

We claim that

\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{1})=n+\frac{C_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}.

(51)

The proof is deferred to the end of this section. It is also clear from the form of
\bm{\Sigma}_{1}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{2}
that they have the same eigenvalues and thus the same determinant. Then the KL-divergence can be simplified to

\mathrm{KL}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma_{2})})=\frac{1}{2}d\left(n+\frac{C_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}-n\right)=\frac{C_{1}^{2}d\epsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}.

This combined with (
49
) and (
50
) finishes the proof.

Proof of (
51
).

For
\gamma_{i}
,
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\bm{Y}_{i}\eqqcolon\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}&\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&(1-\theta)\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}&\sqrt{\theta(1-\theta)}\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\vskip 5.0pt\\
\bm{0}_{n/3\times n/3}&\sqrt{\theta(1-\theta)}\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}&\theta\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

(52)

Then we have that

\bm{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}=\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{\bm{Y}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}d+1}+\frac{\bm{Y}_{1}}{\sigma^{2}d}-\frac{\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{Y}_{1}}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}

(53)

Now we compute the trace of each term separately. It is clear that
\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{I}_{n})=n
.
For
\bm{Y}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, we can see from (
52
) that

\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{Y}_{i})=2\cdot\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{X}_{i}\bm{X}_{i}^{\top})=2\cdot\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{X}_{i}^{\top}\bm{X}_{i})=2\cdot\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{I}_{r})=2r.

(54)

For the trace of
\bm{Y_{2}}\bm{Y_{1}}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{Y}_{1})

\displaystyle=2\cdot\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top})

(55)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top})+\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top})-\mathrm{Trace}\left((\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 2r-2r\|\bm{X}_{1}\bm{X}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{X}_{2}\bm{X}_{2}^{\top}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2r-C_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2},

where the last line comes from (
48
).
Combining (
53
) with (
54
) and (
55
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{1})\leq n-\frac{2r}{\sigma^{2}d+1}+\frac{2r}{\sigma^{2}d}-\frac{2r-C_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}=n+\frac{C_{1}^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}.

D.4
Proof of (
44c
) and (
44d
)

We use generalized Fano’s method. We will construct a hypotheses class
\Gamma\subset\Theta
,
where each
\gamma\in\Gamma
represents a hypothesis consisting
of
\bm{U}^{\star},\bm{U}_{k}^{\star},\bm{V}_{k}^{\star},\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}

for
k\in[K]
. We use the subscript
\gamma
(e.g.
[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}]_{\gamma}
)
to denote the elements associated with the hypothesis

\gamma
. We also use
\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma)}
to denote the probability
distribution of
\{\bm{A}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
induced by (
3
).

Let
\epsilon>0
be a scalar to be specified later.
We will construct
\Gamma
with cardinality
|\Gamma|\geq 2^{r/8}

such that for all
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma
, if
\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}
,

\left\|\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{16}

(56)

and

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma^{\prime})}\right)\leq\frac{4Kd\epsilon\theta}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}+4Ke^{-C_{1}d},

(57)

where
C_{1}
is a universal constant. We will construct
\Gamma

in Section
D.4.1
and prove (
56
)
and (
57
) in Section
D.4.2
.

We now select two different
\epsilon
to prove (
44c
) and (
44d
).

Proof of (
44c
).

Let
\epsilon=r\sigma^{2}/(512K\theta)
. For any
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma
such that
\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}
, as long as
d\geq C_{1}^{-1}\log(512K/r)
,

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)\leq\frac{4Kd\epsilon\theta}{\sigma^{2}d}+4Ke^{-C_{1}d}\leq\frac{r}{128}+4Ke^{-C_{1}d}\leq\frac{\log|\Gamma|}{4}.

Meanwhile,

\left\|\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{1}}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{2}}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{16}\geq\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{128K\theta}.

Then by generalized Fano method (Lemma 3 in
[
Yu97
]
), as long
as
|\Gamma|\geq 16
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{r\sigma^{2}}{128K\theta}}\left(1-\frac{\log|\Gamma|/4+\log 2}{\log|\Gamma|}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma}{50}\sqrt{\frac{r}{K\theta}}.

Proof of (
44d
).

Let
\epsilon=rd\sigma^{4}/(512K\theta)
. For any
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma
such that
\gamma_{1}\neq\gamma_{2}
,
d\geq C_{1}^{-1}\log(512K/r)
,

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{1})}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma_{2})}\right)\leq\frac{4Kd\epsilon\theta}{\sigma^{2}d\cdot\sigma^{2}d}+4Ke^{-C_{1}d}\leq\frac{r}{128}+4Ke^{-C_{1}d}\leq\frac{\log|\Gamma|}{4}.

Meanwhile,

\left\|\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{1}}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma_{2}}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{16}\geq\frac{rd\sigma^{4}}{128K\theta}.

Then by generalized Fano method (Lemma 3 in
[
Yu97
]
), as long
as
|\Gamma|\geq 16
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\bm{U}}}\sup_{\gamma\in\Theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{rd\sigma^{4}}{128K\theta}}\left(1-\frac{\log|\Gamma|/4+\log 2}{\log|\Gamma|}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{50}\sqrt{\frac{rd}{K\theta}}.

D.4.1
Instance construction

Consider the set of binary vectors
\{\bm{h}\in\{-1,1\}^{r}\}
.
Let
H
be a subset of it such that for any
\bm{h}\neq\bm{h}^{\prime}\in H
, the hamming distance
|\{i:h_{i}\neq h^{\prime}_{i}\}|\geq r/8
.
By Gilbert-Varshamov bound (see Theorem 6.21,
[
JJ12
]
),
|H|
can be at least
2^{r/8}
.
In this construction, the parameters in
\Gamma
are indexed by the vectors
\bm{h}
in
H
.
Let
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
, and denote

\delta=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-\epsilon}}{r}
.
We specify the parameters indexed by
\bm{h}
as follows.

For the shared component, define

\bm{U}^{\star}(\bm{h})=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{I}_{r}-\delta r\bm{e}_{1}\bm{e}_{1}^{\top}\\
\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon}{r}}\bm{h}\bm{e}_{1}^{\top}\\
\bm{0}_{r}\\
\bm{0}_{(n-3r)\times r}\end{array}\right].

For the unique component, let
\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}(\bm{h})=\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}(\bm{h})
for odd
k
and
\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}(\bm{h})=\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}(\bm{h})

for even
k
.
Moreover, we set

\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}(\bm{h})=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}-\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon(1-\theta)}{r}}\bm{e}_{1}\bm{h}^{\top}\\
\sqrt{1-\theta}\left(\bm{I}_{r}-\delta\bm{h}\bm{h}^{\top}\right)\\
\sqrt{\theta}\bm{I}_{r}\\
\bm{0}_{(n-3r)\times r}\end{array}\right],\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}(\bm{h})=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}-\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon(1-\theta)}{r}}\bm{e}_{1}\bm{h}^{\top}\\
\sqrt{1-\theta}\left(\bm{I}_{r}-\delta\bm{h}\bm{h}^{\top}\right)\\
-\sqrt{\theta}\bm{I}_{r}\\
\bm{0}_{(n-3r)\times r}\end{array}\right].

In essence, the shared and unique components are all 3-block matrices, with each block having size
r\times r
.

We now verify the first two conditions in (
10
).
It is straightforward with basic algebra to verify the orthogonality condition (
10a
).
For the misalignment condition (
10b
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}(\bm{h})\bm{U}_{k}^{\star\top}(\bm{h})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{U}_{+}^{\star}(\bm{h})\bm{U}_{+}^{\star\top}(\bm{h})+\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}(\bm{h})\bm{U}_{-}^{\star\top}(\bm{h})\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\left\|\bm{U}_{+}^{\star\top}(\bm{h})\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}(\bm{h})\right\|}{2}.

By construction, we have
\bm{U}_{+}^{\star\top}(\bm{h})\bm{U}_{-}^{\star}(\bm{h})=(1-2\theta)\bm{I}_{r}
. Hence the misalignment condition holds.

D.4.2
Proof of (
56
) and (
57
)

Denote
\bm{h},\bm{h}^{\prime}\in H
as the two distinct vectors associated with two hypotheses
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof of (
56
).

Observe that
[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}]_{\gamma}
and
[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}]_{\gamma^{\prime}}
differ only in the first column and they are both projection matrices.
Thus

\displaystyle\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma}-\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right]_{\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}^{\top}\right]_{\gamma}-\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}^{\top}\right]_{\gamma^{\prime}}.

By Lemma 2.6 in
[
CCFM21
]
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}^{\top}\right]_{\gamma}-\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}^{\top}\right]_{\gamma^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\right]_{\gamma}-\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\right]_{\gamma^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}\wedge\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\right]_{\gamma}+\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\right]_{\gamma^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2r}\cdot\left|\{i:h_{i}\neq h^{\prime}_{i}\}\right|\wedge 2(1-\epsilon).

Using
|\{i:h_{i}\neq h^{\prime}_{i}\}\geq r/8
and
\epsilon<1/2
, this can be simplified to

\left\|\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}^{\top}\right]_{\gamma}-\left[\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}\left[\bm{U}^{\star}\right]_{\cdot 1}^{\top}\right]_{\gamma^{\prime}}\right\|^{2}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{16}.

Proof of (
57
).

Let
\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(\gamma)}
be the law of
\bm{A}_{k}
under hypothesis
\gamma

and
\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma)}
be the joint law of
\{\bm{A}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}

under
\gamma
. By independence and symmetry, it follows that for any
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma

\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\mathbb{P}^{(\gamma^{\prime})})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\mathbb{P}_{k}^{(\gamma^{\prime})}\right)=K\cdot\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma^{\prime})}\right).

(58)

Moreover, let
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{(\gamma)}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k}^{(\gamma^{\prime})}
be the
law of
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}
under
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
respectively. Lemma
9
shows that

\mathrm{KL}\left(\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\mathbb{P}_{1}^{(\gamma^{\prime})}\right)\leq\mathrm{KL}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma^{\prime})}\right)+4e^{-C_{1}d},

(59)

where
C_{1}
is a universal constant.

It now suffices to compute
\mathrm{KL}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma^{\prime})})
. Observe that the
j
-th column of
\bm{A}_{k}
is

[\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}]_{\cdot,j}=\bm{U}^{\star}([\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}]_{j,\cdot})^{\top}+\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}([\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}]_{j,\cdot})^{\top}+[\bm{E}_{k}]_{\cdot,j}.

Recall that
\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}
and
\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}
are entrywise i.i.d. Gaussian with covariance
1/d
and
\bm{E}_{k}
are entrywise i.i.d. Gaussian with covariance
1
. Then each column of
\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}
is independent with

[\widetilde{\bm{A}}_{k}]_{\cdot,j}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\bm{0},\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}/d+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}/d+\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n}\right).

The covariance of each column is the same, we may denote

\bm{\Sigma}_{i}\coloneqq\sigma^{2}\left[\bm{I}_{n}+\frac{\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}}{\sigma^{2}d}\right]_{\gamma_{i}}

for
i=1,2
. It is also easy to see that

\bm{\Sigma}_{i}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\left[\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}}{\sigma^{2}d+1}\right]_{\gamma_{i}}.

By independence and the KL-divergence of multivariate Gaussian distributions,

\mathrm{KL}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma^{\prime})})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\left(\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{1})-n-\log\frac{\mathrm{det}(\bm{\Sigma}_{1})}{\mathrm{det}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2})}\right).

We claim that

\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{1})\leq n+\frac{8\epsilon\theta}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}.

(60)

The proof is deferred to the end of this section. It is also clear from the form of
\bm{\Sigma}_{1}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{2}
that they have the same eigenvalues and thus the same determinant. Then the KL-divergence can be simplified to

\mathrm{KL}(\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma)}\parallel\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}^{(\gamma^{\prime})})=\frac{1}{2}d\left(n+\frac{\epsilon\theta}{4\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}-n\right)=\frac{4d\epsilon\theta}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}.

This combined with (
58
) and (
59
) finishes the proof.

Proof of (
60
).

For
\gamma_{i}
,
i=1,2
, denote
\bm{Y}_{i}\eqqcolon\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}+\bm{U}_{1}^{\star}\bm{U}_{1}^{\star\top}
. Then we have that

\bm{\Sigma}_{2}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{1}=\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{\bm{Y}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}d+1}+\frac{\bm{Y}_{1}}{\sigma^{2}d}-\frac{\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{Y}_{1}}{\sigma^{2}d(\sigma^{2}d+1)}

(61)

We now compute the trace of each term separately. It is clear that
\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{I}_{n})=n
.

Without loss of generality consider
\bm{Y}_{1}
. It takes the form

\bm{Y}_{1}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}\begin{array}[]{ccc}\bm{I}_{r}-\epsilon\theta\bm{e}_{1}\bm{e}_{1}^{\top}&\theta\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{r}}\bm{e}_{1}\bm{h}^{\top}&-\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon\theta(1-\theta)}{r}}\bm{e}_{1}\bm{h}^{\top}\\
\theta\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{r}}\bm{h}\bm{e}_{1}^{\top}&(1-\theta)\bm{I}_{r}-\frac{\epsilon\theta}{r}\bm{h}\bm{h}^{\top}&\sqrt{\theta(1-\theta)}(\bm{I}_{r}-\delta\bm{h}\bm{h}^{\top})\\
-\sqrt{\frac{\epsilon\theta(1-\theta)}{r}}\bm{h}\bm{e}_{1}^{\top}&\sqrt{\theta(1-\theta)}(\bm{I}_{r}-\delta\bm{h}\bm{h}^{\top})&\theta\bm{I}_{r}\\
\end{array}&\bm{0}_{3r\times(d-3r)}\\
\hline\cr\bm{0}_{(n-3r)\times 3r}&\bm{0}_{(n-3r)\times(d-3r)}\\
\end{array}\right].

For
\bm{Y}_{i}
,
i=1,2
, we can see from the form of
\bm{Y}_{i}
that

\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{Y}_{i})=(1-\epsilon\theta)+(r-1)+r\cdot(1-\theta+\theta\epsilon/r)+r\theta=2r.

(62)

For the trace of
\bm{Y_{1}}\bm{Y_{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{Y}_{1})

\displaystyle=\left\langle\bm{Y}_{1},\bm{Y}_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=(1-\epsilon\theta)^{2}+(r-1)+\frac{2\theta^{2}\epsilon(1-\epsilon)+2\epsilon\theta(1-\theta)}{r}\langle\bm{h},\bm{h}^{\prime}\rangle+r\cdot(1-\theta)^{2}-2\epsilon\theta(1-\theta)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\epsilon\theta}{r}\right)^{2}\langle\bm{h},\bm{h}^{\prime}\rangle^{2}+2r-4\delta r+2\theta(1-\theta)\delta^{2}\langle\bm{h},\bm{h}^{\prime}\rangle^{2}+r\theta^{2}.

Observe that
\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{Y}_{1}\bm{Y}_{2})=2r
if
\bm{h}=\bm{h}^{\prime}
. Then using the fact that
\langle\bm{h},\bm{h}^{\prime}\rangle\geq-r
and
\langle\bm{h},\bm{h}^{\prime}\rangle^{2}\geq 0
, we can rewrite the above equation as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Trace}(\bm{Y}_{2}\bm{Y}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq 2r-4r\cdot\frac{\theta^{2}\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{r}-4r\cdot\frac{\epsilon\theta(1-\theta)}{r}-r^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{\epsilon\theta}{r}\right)^{2}-2r^{2}\cdot\frac{\epsilon\theta(1-\theta)}{r^{2}}\geq 2r-8\epsilon\theta,

which uses the fact that
\delta r=1-\sqrt{1-\epsilon}\leq\epsilon
.
Combining (
61
) with (
62
) and the above inequality, we have (
60
).

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
3

In this section, we give an analysis that leads to an oracle algorithmic
lower bound. For notational
convenience we use the shorthand
\rho\coloneqq\sigma_{\min}/(\sigma\sqrt{N})
and abuse the big-O notation
O(X)
to indicate any residual term
Y
such that
\|Y\|\leq CX
for some large enough constant
C
.
We prove a more general theorem that assumes
d_{1}=\ldots=d_{k}\eqqcolon d
but
d
can differ from
n
. Theorem
3
is the special case when
n=d
.

Theorem 6
.

Consider
\theta\leq 1/2
. Suppose
n
is large enough,
r\leq n/3
, and
\rho\geq C_{1}

for some large enough constant
C_{1}
.
There exists a configuration of
\bm{U}^{\star}
,
\{\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}_{k=1}^{K}
,

\{\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}\}_{k=1}^{K}
,
\{\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}\}_{k=1}^{K}

such that with probability at least
1-O(KN^{-10})
, the oracle estimator
\widehat{\bm{U}}

output by Algorithm
2
satisfies

\left\|\widehat{\bm{U}}\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\top}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{U}^{\star\top}\right\|\geq C_{2}\sigma^{4}nd-C_{3}\left[\frac{\log N}{\sqrt{K}}\cdot\rho^{-1}+\rho^{-5}\right]

(63)

for some constants
C_{2},C_{3}>0
, with the proviso that
K\geq C_{4}\log^{2}n
for some large constant
C_{4}>0
.

The proof can be summarized as five steps:

1.

We define a configuration of
\bm{U}^{\star}
,
\{\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}\}_{k=1}^{K}
,

\{\bm{V}_{k}^{\star}\}_{k=1}^{K}
,
\{\bm{W}_{k}^{\star}\}_{k=1}^{K}
.

2.

We use the singular subspace expansion in
[
Xia21
]

to identify a fourth-order approximation
\bm{Q}
such that
\bm{M}=\bm{Q}+O(\rho^{-5})
.

3.

We show that SVD on
\mathbb{E}\bm{Q}
results in a biased
estimation of
\bm{U}^{\star}
.

4.

We observe
\bm{Q}\rightarrow\mathbb{E}\bm{Q}
as
K\rightarrow\infty

and deduce that
\bm{M}=\mathbb{E}\bm{Q}+O(\rho^{-5})+O(K^{-1/2}\rho^{-1})
.

5.

We combine Step 3 and 4 to reach the algorithmic
estimation lower bound.

Specifying the configuration.

Without loss of generality assume
k
is even. Let
\bm{U}^{\star}
be an arbitrary orthogonal matrix. For each
k\in[K]
, Define
\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}
to be

\bm{U}_{k}^{\star}=\begin{cases}\sqrt{1-\theta}\bm{Z}_{2}+\sqrt{\theta}\bm{Z}_{3},&k\text{ is odd}\\
\sqrt{1-\theta}\bm{Z}_{2}-\sqrt{\theta}\bm{Z}_{3},&k\text{ is even,}\end{cases}

where
\bm{Z}_{2},\bm{Z}_{3}
